# Midjourney Image Scraper & Dataset Creator

A complete toolkit for scraping Midjourney images, generating captions, and creating HuggingFace datasets with optional automatic upload to HuggingFace Hub.

## 🌟 Features

- **🔍 Web Scraping**: Download images from midjourneysref.com with comprehensive error handling
- **🤖 AI Captioning**: Automatic image captioning using Moondream API with **auto-resume capability**
- **✂️ Smart Cropping**: AI-powered image cropping using OpenAI to optimize aspect ratios
- **🔄 Interrupt-Safe**: Stop and restart any process - it'll pick up where you left off
- **📦 HuggingFace Datasets**: Create professional HF-compatible datasets with metadata
- **☁️ Hub Upload**: Direct upload to HuggingFace Hub with one command
- **📊 Detailed Logging**: Comprehensive feedback and statistics for all operations

## 🚀 Quick Start

### 1. Installation

```bash
# Clone or download this repository
git clone <your-repo-url>
cd style_scraper

# Install all dependencies
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

### 2. Environment Setup

Create a `.env` file in the project root:

```bash
# Required for image captioning
[REDACTED]

# Required for AI-powered cropping
[REDACTED]

# Required for HuggingFace Hub upload
[REDACTED]
```

**Get your tokens:**
- **Moondream API**: https://moondream.ai/api
- **OpenAI API**: https://platform.openai.com/api-keys
- **HuggingFace Hub**: https://huggingface.co/settings/tokens (select "Write" permissions)

### 3. Basic Usage

```bash
# 1. Scrape images
python scraper.py

# 2. Generate captions (optional but recommended)
python caption_images.py

# 3. Crop images with AI analysis (optional)
python crop_dataset.py

# 4. Create HuggingFace dataset
python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts prompts.csv

# 5. Or create and upload in one step
python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts prompts.csv --upload --repo-id your-username/my-dataset
```

## 📖 Detailed Usage

### Step 1: Image Scraping (`scraper.py`)

Downloads images from midjourneysref.com with comprehensive error handling.

```bash
python scraper.py
```

**Features:**
- Downloads images from pages 6-20 by default (configurable in script)
- Pre-flight connectivity testing
- Duplicate detection and skipping
- Detailed error categorization and statistics
- Comprehensive logging to `scraper.log`

**Output:** Images saved to `midjourney_images/` folder

### Step 2: Image Captioning (`caption_images.py`)

Generates captions for uncaptioned images using the Moondream API. **Automatically resumes** from where you left off!

```bash
# Basic usage - automatically resumes from existing captions
python caption_images.py

# First run captions 50 images, then gets interrupted...
# Second run automatically continues with remaining images
python caption_images.py  # No extra flags needed!

# Force recaption all images (ignoring existing captions)
python caption_images.py --recaption

# Custom options
python caption_images.py --images my_images/ --output captions.csv --delay 0.5

# Caption length control
python caption_images.py --prompt-length short    # Concise captions
python caption_images.py --prompt-length normal   # Detailed captions  
python caption_images.py --prompt-length mix      # Random mix (default)
```

**Options:**
- `--images, -i`: Input folder (default: `midjourney_images`)
- `--output, -o`: Output CSV file (default: `prompts.csv`)
- `--existing, -e`: Custom existing prompts file (optional, by default uses output file)
- `--recaption, -r`: Force recaption all images, ignoring existing captions
- `--prompt-length, -l`: Caption length - `short`, `normal`, or `mix` (default: `mix`)
- `--delay, -d`: API rate limiting delay (default: 1.0 seconds)
- `--verbose, -v`: Enable debug logging

**Features:**
- **🔄 Auto-resume**: Automatically skips already-captioned images by default
- **🚀 Interrupt-safe**: Can safely stop and restart the process anytime
- Rate limiting to respect API limits
- API connection testing before processing
- Comprehensive error handling and statistics
- Compatible CSV output for dataset creation

**Output:** CSV file with `filename,prompt` columns

### Step 3: AI-Powered Image Cropping (`crop_dataset.py`)

Analyzes images using OpenAI's vision API to determine optimal crop ratios and creates cropped versions.

```bash
# Basic usage - processes all images in prompts.csv
python crop_dataset.py

# Test with a single image first
python test_crop.py
```

**Features:**
- **🤖 AI Analysis**: Uses OpenAI GPT-4 Vision to analyze each image for optimal cropping
- **📐 Smart Ratios**: Supports 16:9, 9:16, 4:3, 3:4, 1:1, or no cropping
- **🎯 Content-Aware**: Considers image content, composition, and aesthetics
- **📊 Metadata Tracking**: Saves crop ratios and dimensions to updated CSV
- **🔒 Fallback Safe**: Defaults to 1:1 (square) for invalid responses or errors

**Crop Ratios:**
- `16:9` - Wide scenes, landscapes, group shots  
- `9:16` - Tall subjects, full-body portraits, buildings
- `4:3` - Balanced framing, single subjects, general scenes
- `3:4` - Portraits, vertical objects, close-ups
- `1:1` - Square format, where entire image is important
- `no` - Keep original aspect ratio (treated as 1:1)

**Output:**
- `cropped_images/` - Folder with all cropped images
- `prompts_with_crops.csv` - Updated CSV with crop metadata
- `crop_dataset.log` - Detailed processing log

**CSV Columns Added:**
- `crop_ratio` - AI-recommended aspect ratio
- `cropped_width` - Width of cropped image in pixels  
- `cropped_height` - Height of cropped image in pixels
- `cropped_filename` - Filename of the cropped version

### Step 4: Dataset Creation (`create_hf_dataset.py`)

Creates a professional HuggingFace-compatible dataset with metadata extraction.

```bash
# Local dataset only
python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts prompts.csv

# Using cropped images and metadata
python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts prompts_with_crops.csv --input cropped_images

# With HuggingFace Hub upload
python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts prompts.csv --upload --repo-id username/dataset-name
```

**Options:**
- `--input, -i`: Input images folder (default: `midjourney_images`)
- `--output, -o`: Output dataset folder (default: `midjourney_hf_dataset`)
- `--name, -n`: Dataset name (default: `midjourney-images`)
- `--prompts, -p`: Path to prompts CSV file
- `--upload, -u`: Upload to HuggingFace Hub
- `--repo-id, -r`: HuggingFace repository ID (e.g., `username/dataset-name`)
- `--verbose, -v`: Enable debug logging

**Features:**
- Extracts comprehensive image metadata (resolution, file size, orientation, etc.)
- Style reference (`sref`) extraction from filenames
- HuggingFace-compatible structure and configuration
- Automatic README generation with usage examples
- Dataset loading scripts for seamless integration
- Optional direct upload to HuggingFace Hub

## 📊 Output Structure

### Dataset Directory Structure
```
midjourney_hf_dataset/
├── images/                    # All image files
├── metadata/
│   ├── metadata.csv          # Main HuggingFace metadata
│   ├── metadata_detailed.json  # Detailed metadata
│   └── dataset_summary.json  # Dataset statistics
├── dataset_config/           # HuggingFace configuration
│   ├── dataset_infos.json
│   └── midjourney-images.py  # Dataset loading script
└── README.md                 # Generated documentation
```

### Metadata Fields

Each image includes comprehensive metadata:

- `filename`: Original image filename
- `file_path`: Relative path within dataset
- `sref`: Style reference ID (extracted from filename)
- `prompt`: AI-generated or provided caption
- `width`: Image width in pixels  
- `height`: Image height in pixels
- `file_size_mb`: File size in megabytes
- `size_category`: Resolution category (high/medium/low)
- `orientation`: Image orientation (landscape/portrait/square)

## 🔧 Configuration

### Scraper Configuration

Edit variables in `scraper.py`:

```python
# Folder where you want to save the images
DOWNLOAD_FOLDER = "midjourney_images"

# Page range to scrape
start_page = 6
end_page = 20
```

### Caption Configuration

The captioning script supports rate limiting, automatic resuming, and caption length control:

```bash
# Caption length options
python caption_images.py --prompt-length short    # Short, concise captions
python caption_images.py --prompt-length normal   # Detailed descriptions
python caption_images.py --prompt-length mix      # Random mix (default - adds variety)

# Rate limiting for API stability
python caption_images.py --delay 1.0

# Auto-resume from previous run (default behavior)
python caption_images.py

# Force recaption everything from scratch
python caption_images.py --recaption

# Use a different existing file for comparison
python caption_images.py --existing other_captions.csv
```

### Dataset Configuration

Customize dataset creation:

```bash
# Custom dataset name and structure
python create_hf_dataset.py \
  --input my_images/ \
  --output my_custom_dataset/ \
  --name "my-custom-midjourney-dataset" \
  --prompts captions.csv
```

## 🤗 Using Your Dataset

### Loading from HuggingFace Hub

```python
from datasets import load_dataset

# Load your uploaded dataset
dataset = load_dataset("username/your-dataset-name")

# Access data
for example in dataset["train"]:
    image = example["image"]           # PIL Image
    prompt = example["prompt"]         # Caption text
    sref = example["sref"]            # Style reference
    width = example["width"]          # Image width
    # ... other metadata
```

### Loading Locally

```python
import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
import os

# Load metadata
metadata = pd.read_csv("midjourney_hf_dataset/metadata/metadata.csv")

# Load specific image
def load_image(filename):
    return Image.open(f"midjourney_hf_dataset/images/{filename}")

# Filter by criteria
high_res = metadata[metadata['size_category'] == 'high_resolution']
has_prompts = metadata[metadata['prompt'] != ""]
same_style = metadata[metadata['sref'] == '4160600070']
```

## 📈 Advanced Usage

### Batch Processing

```bash
# Process multiple scraping sessions - auto-resumes captioning!
for i in {1..5}; do
  python scraper.py
  python caption_images.py  # Automatically skips existing captions
done

# Create final dataset
python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts prompts.csv --upload --repo-id username/large-midjourney-dataset

# Alternative: Force fresh captions for each batch
for i in {1..5}; do
  python scraper.py
  python caption_images.py --recaption
done
```

### Custom Prompts

You can provide your own prompts instead of using AI captioning:

```csv
filename,prompt
4160600070-1-d9409ee5.png,"A majestic dragon soaring over snow-capped mountains"
4160600070-2-a8b7c9d2.png,"Cyberpunk cityscape with neon reflections in rain"
```

### Style-Based Datasets

```bash
# Filter by style reference before creating dataset
python -c "
import pandas as pd
df = pd.read_csv('prompts.csv')
style_df = df[df['filename'].str.startswith('4160600070')]
style_df.to_csv('style_specific_prompts.csv', index=False)
"

python create_hf_dataset.py --prompts style_specific_prompts.csv --name "style-4160600070"
```

## 🐛 Troubleshooting

### Common Issues

**1. Missing API Keys**
```
Error: MOONDREAM_API_KEY not found
```
- Ensure `.env` file exists with valid API key
- Check API key has sufficient credits

**2. HuggingFace Upload Fails**
```
Error: HF_TOKEN not found
```
- Create token at https://huggingface.co/settings/tokens
- Ensure "Write" permissions are selected
- Check repository name is available

**3. No Images Found**
```
Warning: No images found with primary selector
```
- Website structure may have changed
- Check internet connection
- Verify target pages exist

**4. Caption Generation Fails**
```
Failed to caption image: API error
```
- Check Moondream API status
- Verify API key and credits
- Reduce rate limiting with `--delay`
- The script auto-resumes, so you can safely restart after fixing the issue

**5. Want to Recaption Existing Images**
```
Images already have captions but I want to regenerate them
```
- Use `--recaption` flag to ignore existing captions
- Or delete the existing CSV file to start fresh

### Log Files

Check these log files for detailed debugging:
- `scraper.log`: Web scraping logs
- `caption_images.log`: Captioning process logs  
- `hf_dataset_creation.log`: Dataset creation logs

Based on the search results, here's what I found about DataClaw and Peter [REDACTED]'s announcement:

DataClaw parses session logs, redacts secrets and PII, and uploads the result as a ready-to-use dataset. It allows users to turn their Claude Code, Codex, and Gemini CLI conversation history into structured data and publish it to Hugging Face with a single command.

The project is described as a performance art project, framed as a response to Anthropic's data policies, with DataClaw letting users "throw the ladder back down."

One announcement referenced that after Deepseek was called out for scraping 150k Claude messages, peteromallet released 155k of his Claude Code messages with Opus 4.5.

Every export is tagged dataclaw on Hugging Face. The tool has gained significant attention, with the GitHub repository having 545 stars and 75 forks.

Peter Omallet shared a beta of his new open source project called ComfyUI Creative Interpolation, which allows you to interpolate between a batch of images with Animatediff to fill in gaps with dynamic, precisely controllable motion.

These are some of the notable open source projects that Peter Omallet has built and released according to his X posts.

Based on the search results, I found information about peteromallet's LoRA models on Hugging Face:

## peteromallet's LoRA Models

peteromallet has created InScene and InScene Annotate, which are a pair of LoRA fine-tunes for QwenEdit that enhance its ability to generate images based on scene references. The InScene and InScene Annotate LoRAs were trained on curated datasets focusing on scene composition and spatial relationships.

InScene is trained on pairs of different shots within the same scene, along with prompts describing the desired output.

Additionally, the InScene LoRA for Flux.Kontext was trained on 394 image pairs, with this dataset created by extracting and enriching frames from the WebVid dataset. InScene is a LoRA for Flux.Kontext.dev that's designed to generate images that maintain scene consistency with a source image.

peteromallet also has other LoRA models, including 3 Motion LoRAs for Animatediff: WAS26 (trained on selections of art shared in the Banodoco Discord), Smoooth (trained on videos with smooth motion), and LiquidAF (trained on liquid simulations).

I found information about Peter O'Mallet's X profile (@peteromallet). Here's what the search results show:

POM (@peteromallet) is involved with Open Source AI Art, @banodoco (Tools for creativity), and @Reigh_Art/@hannahsubmarine, based in Berlin/Ireland, and joined X in December 2011.

Based on the search results, Peter has been active in several areas:

1. **Desloppify Project**: He's been sharing Desloppify, an agent toolset for making code beautiful, and added the ability to find security issues.

2. **Open Source AI Art**: He's been open sourcing InScene and InScene Annotate - LoRAs for steering in-scene image generation with QwenEdit, using prompts and annotations to generate consistent images.

3. **OpenMuse.ai**: He spent months building OpenMuse.ai, growing from the Banodoco community, with hopes it would become a place for the open source AI art movement.

4. **Community Engagement**: He's highlighted community members like Kijai, who went from never writing code to becoming one of the most productive engineers in the open source AI art ecosystem.

Based on the search results, I found information about the "poms-funtime-mlora-emporium" repository by peteromallet:

Peteromallet trained 3 Motion LoRAs for Animatediff: WAS26 (trained on selections of art shared in the Banodoco Discord), Smoooth (trained on videos with smooth motion), and LiquidAF (trained on liquid simulations). 

The poms-funtime-mlora-emporium repository is 406 MB in size and contains the three motion LoRA files: LiquidAF-0-1.safetensors, Smoooth-0-1.safetensors, and WAS26.safetensors, each approximately 135 MB.

The repository is hosted on Hugging Face at `huggingface.co/peteromallet/poms-funtime-mlora-emporium` and contains these Motion LoRAs that can be used with the AnimateDiff extension for video generation.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about peteromallet's Twitter activity related to Wan, LTXV, headless, and video generation:

## Key Finding

Peter Omallet shared a fork on X that works headlessly with highly VRAM-optimised Wan (with VACE!) and LTXV available to build on top of or run in the command line.

## Related Project: WanGP

The search results show that Peter Omallet is associated with WanGP 5.2, which builds on the work of @deepbeepmeep. WanGP supports the Wan (and derived models) but also Hunyuan Video, Flux, Qwen, Z-Image, LongCat, Kandinsky, LTXV, LTX-2, and other models with low VRAM requirements.

Users can create their queue in the web UI, save it with "Save Queue", then process it headless with CLI Documentation available for details.

This appears to be an optimization-focused project for running advanced video generation models like Wan and LTXV efficiently on consumer hardware with headless (command-line) support.

Based on the search results, here are the key findings:

**Peter Omallet's Work with WebVid:**

Peter Omallet's InScene LoRA dataset was created by extracting and enriching frames from the WebVid dataset.

**WebVid Dataset:**

WebVid is a large-scale dataset of short videos with textual descriptions sourced from the web. It contains 10.7M video-caption pairs and 52K total video hours.

**Related Projects:**

Peter Omallet has several related projects:

1. **Magnetron** - A tool designed to collect key pairs from videos and tag them using an automated process with human review at the end, which can be used to extract full videos from the WebVid source.

2. **DataClaw** - A tool that parses session logs, redacts secrets and PII, and uploads the result as a ready-to-use dataset.

The search results didn't return specific information about a "parser" tool specifically named in relation to WebVid and Twitter, though the Magnetron project appears to be a parsing/processing tool for WebVid content.

# InData Project Summary

**Project Name:** InData - AI Dataset Processing Pipeline

**Description:** A Supabase-based system for processing and managing AI-generated image datasets.

## What It Does

The project provides an end-to-end pipeline for handling AI-generated imagery:

- **AI Processing**: Generates image variations using an automated pipeline (`ai_dataset_processor.py`)
- **Review Interface**: Offers a Streamlit web application for human evaluation of generated images
- **Database Management**: Integrates with Supabase for storing and retrieving dataset information
- **Content Filtering**: Uses Groq API integration for LLM-based content analysis and prompt generation

## Key Components

- **scripts/**: Core processing scripts including dataset backup and Hugging Face integration
- **apps/**: User-facing Streamlit review application
- **utils/**: Reusable modules for AI configuration, LLM operations, and database interactions
- **supabase/**: Database configuration and migrations

## Tech Stack

Python (68.8%), TypeScript (26.2%), PLpgSQL (4.5%), plus Shell, JavaScript, and CSS

## Quick Start

Installation requires copying environment variables, installing dependencies, running the processing pipeline, then launching the review interface via Streamlit.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about peteromallet's announcements regarding InScene Annotate, InSubject, and Qwen:

## InSubject
InSubject 0.5 is a QwenEdit LoRA trained for creating highly consistent characters and objects with just a single image reference. QwenEdit InSubject significantly improves QwenEdit's ability to preserve subjects while making edits to images, and works effectively with both single subjects and multiple subjects in the same image.

## InScene and InScene Annotate
InScene and InScene Annotate are a pair of LoRA fine-tunes for QwenEdit that enhance its ability to generate images based on scene references. InScene uses pairs of different shots within the same scene, while InScene Annotate uses annotated images with green rectangle markers. Both models are currently in beta and will be improved significantly over time.

The search results primarily show information from Hugging Face repositories and X/Twitter highlights rather than a specific announcement thread. The models appear to be actively used and documented within the AI image editing community.

# QwenEdit InStyle LoRA

## Description

**QwenEdit InStyle** is a LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) fine-tune for the QwenEdit model that enhances its ability to generate images based on style references. It improves upon the base model's style transfer capabilities by better capturing style nuances and avoiding unwanted detail transplantation from input images.

## What It Does

- Generates images based on style reference images
- Captures subtle style nuances from reference images
- Avoids unwanted detail transplantation from source images
- Generates coherent images that match the intended style
- Maintains strong prompt adherence

## How to Use

### Prompt Format
Start prompts with: `Make an image in this style of`

Example: `Make an image in this style of a serene mountain landscape at sunset.`

### Code Example (Diffusers)

```python
import torch
from diffusers import QwenImageEditPipeline

pipe = QwenImageEditPipeline.from_pretrained("Qwen/Qwen-Image-Edit", torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16)
pipe.to("cuda")

pipe.load_lora_weights("peteromallet/Qwen-Image-Edit-InStyle", weight_name="InStyle-0.5.safetensors")
```

## Training Data

Trained on a curated dataset of high-quality Midjourney style references with diverse artistic styles and clean style-content separation.

**Public Dataset:** https://huggingface.co/datasets/peteromallet/high-quality-midjouney-srefs

## Strengths & Weaknesses

**Strengths:**
- Subtle style nuance capture
- Clean detail separation
- Coherent style-matched generation
- Strong prompt adherence

**Weaknesses:**
- May struggle with extremely abstract styles
- Conflicts between specific technical details and reference styles
- Occasional anatomy issues

## Links

- **Model:** https://huggingface.co/peteromallet/Qwen-Image-Edit-InStyle
- **Dataset:** https://huggingface.co/datasets/peteromallet/high-quality-midjouney-srefs
- **Base Model:** Qwen/Qwen-Image-Edit

## Metadata

- **Downloads (last month):** 3,955
- **License:** Apache 2.0
- **Task:** Image-to-Image
- **Library:** Diffusers
```

Based on the search results, here's what I found about peteromallet's InStyle LoRA sharing:

InStyle is a LoRA for Qwen-Edit that allows it to generate images based on a style reference. The base model can attempt this task but has frequent issues, and this LoRA helps it properly understand styles and avoid non-style references to the input.

The search results show multiple posts from peteromallet sharing this LoRA, with the main posts appearing on X (formerly Twitter). In addition to InStyle, the results also show that peteromallet has shared other LoRAs for various AI models, including InScene and InScene Annotate - a pair of LoRAs for steering in-scene image generation with QwenEdit, which allow you to use a combination of prompts and annotations to generate consistent images inside a scene.

### 1. ComfyUI Creative Interpolation

- **What it does:** An open-source ComfyUI node that allows interpolation between a batch of images using AnimateDiff, filling in the gaps with dynamic, precisely controllable motion. This was the predecessor to / evolved into Steerable Motion.
- **Tweet:** "Sharing a beta of my new open source project: ComfyUI Creative Interpolation! It's allows you to interpolate between a batch of images w/ Animatediff - filling in the gaps with dynamic, precisely controllable motion."
- **Tweet URL:** [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1724048958308188172](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1724048958308188172)
- **Date:** Approximately November 2023 (based on tweet ID chronology)
- **Repo:** https://github.com/peteromallet/ComfyUI-Creative-Interpolation (referenced via t.co link)
- **Media:** Attached images/video showing the interpolation examples

---

### 2. Pom's Funtime mLoRA Emporium (3 AnimateDiff Motion LoRAs)

- **What it does:** Three custom-trained Motion LoRAs for AnimateDiff:
  1. **WAS26** - trained on selections of art shared in the Banodoco Discord
  2. **Smoooth** - trained on videos with smooth motion
  3. **LiquidAF** - trained on liquid simulations
- **Tweet:** "I trained 3 Motion LoRAs for Animatediff: 1) WAS26: trained on selections of art shared in the Banodoco Discord 2) Smoooth: trained on videos w/ smooth motion 3) LiquidAF: trained on liquid simulations"
- **Tweet URL:** [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1776041087355724179](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1776041087355724179)
- **Date:** Approximately April 2024
- **Hugging Face:** https://huggingface.co/peteromallet/poms-funtime-mlora-emporium
- **Media:** Attached examples of the LoRAs in action

---

### 3. OpenMuse.ai

- **What it does:** A platform/home for the open source AI art movement, grown from the Banodoco community. Intended to be a place people find inspiring and empowering.
- **Tweet (main announcement):** Announced he spent months building OpenMuse.ai. Credit for the name goes to @cubiq.
- **Tweet URLs:**
  - [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1921596686113083430](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1921596686113083430) (main announcement)
  - [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1921597110572470402](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1921597110572470402) (link share)
  - [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1921607793619902939](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1921607793619902939) (credit to @cubiq for naming)
- **Date:** Approximately May 11, 2025
- **Repo:** https://github.com/banodoco/openmuse
- **Website:** OpenMuse.ai

---

### 4. The Arca Gidan Prize

- **What it does:** An art competition focused on open-source AI models, supported by ComfyUI and Banodoco. The goal is to push open art models to their artistic limits. Winners fly to Hollywood to show their work to industry leaders and open source enthusiasts.
- **Tweet:** "Voting is happening for the first edition of The Arca Gidan Prize! There's some extremely impressive work there from artists who are using open models to truly carve their own paths."
- **Tweet URL:** [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1985467117349847194](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1985467117349847194)
- **Date:** Approximately late 2025
- **Website:** https://arcagidan.com/
- **Discord:** https://discord.com/invite/Yj7DRvckRu
- **Instagram announcement:** https://www.instagram.com/reel/DQSINgNDPjC/
- **Media:** Trailer video attached to the tweet
- **Note:** First winner was Akshay Kakade for the AI film "Woven," shown at Mack Sennett Studios in Hollywood (November 2025)

---

### 5. Headless Wan2GP Fork (VRAM-optimized Wan + LTXV)

- **What it does:** A fork of deepbeepmeep's Wan2GP that works headlessly (command-line), featuring highly VRAM-optimized Wan (with VACE!) and LTXV video generation models. Can be set up with a single command.
- **Tweet:** "Sharing a fork of this that works headlessly! Highly VRAM-optimised Wan (w/ VACE!) and LTXV available to build on top of or run in the command line! Just a single command & it's all set up, thanks mostly to the work of @deepbeepmeep"
- **Tweet URL:** [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1925030085356089677](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1925030085356089677)
- **Date:** Approximately May 2025
- **Link:** Referenced via t.co link in tweet
- **Media:** Attached images/video

---

### 6. InStyle (QwenEdit LoRA for Style Transfer)

- **What it does:** A LoRA fine-tune for QwenEdit that generates images based on a style reference. Trained on a curated dataset of high-quality Midjourney style references. Improves the base model's ability to understand styles and avoid non-style references. (Note: This is distinct from the Qwen Image Edit LoRAs in your list if that only covers InScene/InSubject -- InStyle is a separate LoRA.)
- **Tweet (announcement):** "Sharing InStyle, a LoRA for Qwen-Edit that allows it to generate images based on a style reference. The base model can attempt this task but has frequent issues. This LoRA helps it properly understand styles + avoid non-style references to the input."
- **Tweet URL:** [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1961510115632452068](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1961510115632452068)
- **Follow-up tweet (comparison):** [https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1962223444314992759](https://x.com/peteromallet/status/1962223444314992759) -- comparison between Nano Banana + QwenEdit + InStyle for style transfer
- **Hugging Face:** https://huggingface.co/peteromallet/Qwen-Image-Edit-InStyle (3,955 monthly downloads, 92 likes)
- **Dataset:** https://huggingface.co/datasets/peteromallet/high-quality-midjouney-srefs

---

### 7. InSubject (QwenEdit LoRA for Subject Preservation)

- **What it does:** A QwenEdit LoRA trained for creating highly consistent characters and objects with just a single image reference. Improves QwenEdit's ability to preserve subjects while making edits. (Again, distinct from InScene if your list only covers InScene.)
- **Hugging Face:** https://huggingface.co/peteromallet/Qwen-Image-Edit-InSubject (4,590 monthly downloads, 83 likes)
- **Dataset:** https://huggingface.co/datasets/peteromallet/InSubject-Dataset
- **Note:** I did not find a distinct announcement tweet for InSubject sharing, but it exists as a separate published model.

---

### 8. Brain of BDNC (Banodoco Discord Bot)

- **What it does:** A community bot for the Banodoco Discord that shares curated content to external platforms like Twitter and answers questions about past discussions using the community's conversation history.
- **Repo:** https://github.com/banodoco/brain-of-bdnc (also at https://github.com/peteromallet/hivemind/)
- **No specific standalone announcement tweet found** -- appears to be an internal community project.

### 9. InData

- **What it does:** A Supabase-based AI dataset processing pipeline for processing and managing AI-generated image datasets. Features AI processing (generates image variations), a Streamlit review interface for human evaluation, database management with Supabase, and content filtering with Groq API.
- **Repo:** https://github.com/peteromallet/InData
- **Tech stack:** Python (68.8%), TypeScript (26.2%), PLpgSQL (4.5%)
- **Updated:** December 8, 2025
- **No specific announcement tweet found.**

Key findings about Deforum:

- Its primary tool is the Deforum Web UI extension, which can be integrated into the Automatic Web UI and offers advanced functionalities, such as video style transfer, motion effects, and frame upscaling.

- Deforum Stable Diffusion has additional features such as animation in the form of 2D and 3D, Video Init, and a few other masking options.

- The tool is available in multiple formats: there are four ways to run Deforum Stable Diffusion: locally with the .py file, locally with Jupyter, locally through Colab, and on Colab servers.

# Dough: AI Video Animation Tool

## What It Is
Dough is an open-source application designed for crafting videos with AI while maintaining granular control over the creative output. The tool enables users to guide video generation through images and example videos.

## Primary Functions
The platform facilitates a three-stage workflow:
1. **Image Generation**: Users create hundreds of styled images via the "Inspiration Engine"
2. **Shot Assembly**: Frames are organized into editable shots
3. **Animation**: Videos are produced with frame-by-frame motion control and Motion LoRAs (specialized adapters)

## Key Technologies
The README indicates the stack includes Streamlit, ComfyUI, and Python-based infrastructure. The tool integrates two primary steering mechanisms: *Steerable Motion* and *Motion Director* (from AnimateDiff).

## Installation & Requirements
- Minimum VRAM: 12GB
- Supported platforms: Linux and Windows
- Recommended installation via Pinokio browser or manual setup
- Requires Python 3.10

## Notable Absence
The README contains no mention of Stable Diffusion 1.5, keyframes, or the tool's creation date. There are no references to predecessor tools or version history beyond the current implementation.

The project appears relatively recent based on the repository structure (1,344 commits as of archival on December 29, 2024), though exact origins aren't documented in this README.
```

# Steerable Motion: Historical Context & Predecessors

## Project Origins
**Creation Date:** November 11, 2023

## Predecessor Tools & Technologies

Steerable Motion builds upon several established frameworks rather than being a completely novel creation:

### For the Animatediff Approach:
- **IPAdapter_plus** by Cubiq - The code draws heavily from this tool
- **AnimateDiff Evolved** by Kosinkadink - Used in workflows
- **ComfyUI-Advanced-ControlNet** by Kosinkadink - Integrated into the system
- **Fizznodes** by Fizzledorf - Incorporated for node functionality
- **Frame Interpolation** by Fannovel16 - Utilized for video processing

### For the Wan Approach:
- **ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper** by Kijai - Described as "wonderful" and foundational to this implementation
- VACE and Wan teams' original research

## Key Philosophical Context

The developers emphasize that Steerable Motion functions "more like a paint brush - an artistic tool" rather than an out-of-the-box solution. This positions it as an evolution of video generation techniques that require user expertise and experimentation to master effectively.

The project represents a synthesis of multiple existing ComfyUI node ecosystems and AI animation frameworks into a cohesive system for image-based video steering.

Based on the web search results, I found relevant information about your query:

## Dough by Banodoco

Dough is a open source tool for steering AI animations with precision. The project is hosted on GitHub at banodoco/Dough.

## Key Features

The application includes a frame mover that allows users to rapidly move and edit individual frames and batches of frames. Users can load motion data (individual frame settings, prompt, strength etc.) from previous generations to quickly iterate between different settings.

## Video Generation Capabilities

Users can now generate interpolated videos using dynamicrafter (suitable for realistic styles) and have the option to generate videos over twice as fast, though this involves some detail loss. The tool also supports upscaling of generated videos to higher resolutions.

## Interface and Workflow

The tool allows users to add video generations to a backlog that won't run right away, and users can start them using the "Run Backlog" button to focus on work and run all backlogs at the end.

The search results show that Dough is built as an open-source interface for AI video generation with keyframe-based animation control, similar to other Streamlit-based video generation tools, though the results don't specifically detail all the technical integration with Stable Diffusion or the exact 2023 release information you were searching for.

# Magnetron Repository Overview

## Purpose
Magnetron is a specialized data collection tool designed to gather and annotate motion data from videos for training video generation models, particularly for projects like Steerable Motion.

## Core Functionality
The tool extracts "key pairs" (frame pairs) from videos and applies automated tagging with human review validation. Users can leverage this tagged data to train models or extract corresponding video segments from the Webvid dataset source.

## Technical Stack
This is a **Python-based project** (100% Python) that includes:

- **Main application**: Streamlit-based web interface (`magnetron.py`)
- **Review system**: Discord bot for human validation (`review_bot.py`)
- **Data export**: CSV output capability (`output.csv`)
- **Configuration**: Modular config directory

## Required Dependencies
The project requires several API integrations:
- OpenAI (vision/language analysis)
- Replicate (model inference)
- AWS (storage/cloud services)
- Discord (collaborative review)

## Is It AI Art Related?
Yes—it's explicitly designed for training video generation models. It's not itself a generation tool, but rather an infrastructure project for creating high-quality training datasets with precisely-labeled motion sequences.

The repository has modest engagement (9 stars, 2 forks) and actively seeks collaborators among ML engineers and artists.
```

# Dough Release History

Based on the available GitHub releases page, here are the documented releases:

## Latest Releases Listed

**Dough 0.9.9** (June 30, 2024)
- Added Pinokio support for installation discovery
- Introduced Inspiration Engine for enhanced prompt and style control
- Enabled local Stable Diffusion 3 Medium execution
- Custom ComfyUI path configuration
- Health check system for models and nodes
- Upscaled video aggregation page
- Generation details display with independent settings/image loading
- Cancel and backlog generation controls
- Re-use last settings functionality

**Dough 0.9.0** (May 3, 2024)
- Updated "Load settings" option to maintain single image versions
- Custom resolution support added to Project Settings
- Project deletion capability
- SD3 API integration with personal API keys
- New Steerable Motion flow for large image batches
- Various UX improvements

**Dough 0.8.3** (April 13, 2024)
- High-detail mode implementing Steerable Motion 1.3
- WAS26 Motion LoRA for smoother motion
- Auto-updater functionality

**Dough 0.7.0** (March 24, 2024)
- Video upscaler feature
- Motion LoRA training capability
- Load images from previous generations
- Fast frame moving and editing tools

**Dough 0.5.1** (March 9, 2024 - Pre-release)
- Motion data loading from previous generations
- Backlog feature for video generation queuing
- Quick setup scripts
- Multiple aspect ratio support

## Notable Absence

The releases page does **not contain** information about earlier versions, predecessors with alternative names, or explicit mentions of Stable Diffusion 1.5 implementation. The earliest accessible release documented is version 0.5.1.

I've completed the web search for your query. The results show several Stable Diffusion video generation tools and techniques that involve keyframes, including:

1. **Stable Diffusion Video Maker** - A tool that can insert different numbers of frames between keyframes, which can be animated using different curves.

2. **Deforum and related tools** - Tools for Stable Diffusion animations as of April 2023 for the #DiffuseTogether competition, including AnimationPreview that can generate and quickly visualize key frame camera movement.

3. **EbSynth-based workflows** - The basic idea involves picking keyframes across the video, stylizing them with image-to-image, and using them as references to paint adjacent frames, a method pioneered by EbSynth.

4. **AnimateDiff** - A technique that generates key frames from text prompts using the diffusion model, then uses a motion module to interpolate intermediate frames between these keys.

        lines = [line.split(',') for line in f.read().splitlines()]

    for i in range(1, 24):

        key_settings[lines[i][11]] = lines[i][12]

    return key_settings

def create_working_assets(video_name):

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets")

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames")

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames/0_extracted")
    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames/1_character_pipeline_completed")
    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames/2_backdrop_pipeline_completed")

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/resources")

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/resources/backgrounds")
    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/resources/masks")
    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/resources/music")
    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/resources/training_data")

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/videos")

    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/videos/0_raw")
    os.mkdir("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/videos/1_final")

def update_key_setting(key, pair_value, project_name):
    
    csv_file_path = f'videos/{project_name}/settings.csv'
    
    with open(csv_file_path, 'r') as csv_file:
        csv_reader = csv.reader(csv_file)

        rows = []

        for row in csv_reader:
            if row[11] == key:            
                row_number = csv_reader.line_num - 2
                print(row_number)
                new_value = pair_value        
    
    # open the csv file in write mode
    # Load the CSV file into a pandas DataFrame
    df = pd.read_csv(csv_file_path)

    # Update the value on the 10th row of the 12th column
    df.iat[row_number, 12] = new_value

    # Save the updated DataFrame to a new CSV file
    df.to_csv(csv_file_path, index=False)


def get_timing_details(video_name):

    timing_details = []

    with open(("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/settings.csv"), 'r') as f:

        lines = [line.split(',') for line in f.read().splitlines()]

    number_of_rows = (len)(lines)


    for i in range(1, number_of_rows):        

        current_frame = {}

        current_frame["frame_time"] = lines[i][1]

        if current_frame["frame_time"] != "":
            
            current_frame["total_duration_of_clip"] = ""
            current_frame["model_id"] = lines[i][2]
            current_frame["prompt"] = lines[i][3]
            current_frame["duration_of_static_time"] = lines[i][4]
            current_frame["duration_of_morph_time"] = ""
            current_frame["background"] = lines[i][8]
            current_frame["mask"] = lines[i][9]
            current_frame["notes"] = lines[i][5]
            current_frame["whats_happening"] = lines[i][6]
            current_frame["shot"] = lines[i][7]

            timing_details.append(current_frame)

    return timing_details


def calculate_frame_number_at_time(input_video, time_of_frame):

    time_of_frame = float(time_of_frame)

    video = cv2.VideoCapture(input_video)

    frame_count = float(video.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT))

    fps = int(video.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FPS))

    length_of_video = float(frame_count / fps)

    percentage_of_video = float(time_of_frame / length_of_video)

    frame_number = int(percentage_of_video * frame_count)

    if frame_number == 0:
        frame_number = 1

    return frame_number



def extract_all_frames(input_video, project_name, timing_details, time_per_frame):

    folder = 'videos/' + str(project_name) + '/assets/frames/0_extracted'

    for filename in os.listdir(folder):
        os.remove(os.path.join(folder, filename))

    update_key_setting('time_per_frame', time_per_frame, project_name)

    key_settings = get_key_settings("videos/" + str(project_name) + "/settings.csv")

    timing_details = get_timing_details(input_video,float(key_settings["time_per_frame"]), key_settings["model_id"],key_settings["master_prompt"], int(key_settings["static_time"]), key_settings["background_image"], key_settings["custom_or_stable"], project_name)

    for i in timing_details:

        index_of_current_item = timing_details.index(i)
    
        time_of_frame = float(timing_details[index_of_current_item]["frame_time"])

        extract_frame_number = calculate_frame_number_at_time(input_video, time_of_frame)

        extract_frame(index_of_current_item, project_name, input_video, extract_frame_number)

def extract_frame(frame_number, video_name, input_video_file, extract_frame_number):

    input_video_file = cv2.VideoCapture(input_video_file)

    input_video_file.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_POS_FRAMES, extract_frame_number)

    ret, frame = input_video_file.read()

    cv2.imwrite("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames/0_extracted/" + str(frame_number) + ".png", frame)

    img = Image.open("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames/0_extracted/" + str(frame_number) + ".png")

    img.save("videos/" + video_name + "/assets/frames/0_extracted/" + str(frame_number) + ".png")

    print(f"{frame_number}.png extracted!")

    return str(frame_number) + ".png"

def touch_up_images(video_name, replicate_api_key, index_of_current_item, type_of_touch_up, custom_touch_up_query, dreamstudio_ai_api_key, original_prompt):

    os.environ["REPLICATE_API_TOKEN"] = replicate_api_key

    model = replicate.models.get("tencentarc/gfpgan")

    image = "videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/1_character_pipeline_completed/" + str(index_of_current_item) + ".png"

    output = model.predict(img=open(image, "rb"))

    try:

        urllib.request.urlretrieve(output, image)

    except Exception as e:

        print("Error:")

def resize_image(video_name, image_number, new_width,new_height):

    image = Image.open("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/1_character_pipeline_completed/" + str(image_number) + ".png")

    resized_image = image.resize((new_width, new_height))

    resized_image.save("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/1_character_pipeline_completed/" + str(image_number) + ".png")

    return resized_image

def face_swap(replicate_api_key, video_name, index_of_current_item,stablediffusionapi_com_api_key):
    
    os.environ["REPLICATE_API_TOKEN"] = replicate_api_key

    model = replicate.models.get("arielreplicate/ghost_face_swap")

    version = model.versions.get("106df0aaf9690354379d8cd291ad337f6b3ea02fe07d90feb1dafd64820066fa")

    source_face = upload_image("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/face.png", stablediffusionapi_com_api_key)

    target_face = upload_image("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/0_extracted/" + str(index_of_current_item) + ".png", stablediffusionapi_com_api_key)

    output = version.predict(source_path=source_face, target_path=target_face,use_sr=0)

    new_image = "videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/1_character_pipeline_completed/" + str(index_of_current_item) + ".png"
    
    try:

        urllib.request.urlretrieve(output, new_image)

    except Exception as e:

        print(e)

def prompt_model_stability(videoname, image_number, prompt, dreamstudio_ai_api_key, apend_to_prompt):

    os.environ['STABILITY_HOST'] = 'grpc.stability.ai:443'

    os.environ['STABILITY_KEY'] = dreamstudio_ai_api_key

    # Set up our connection to the API.
    stability_api = client.StabilityInference(
        key=os.environ['STABILITY_KEY'],  # API Key reference.
        verbose=True,  # Print debug messages.
        engine="stable-diffusion-v1-5",
    )

    with open("videos/" + videoname + "/assets/frames/2_background_added/" + str(image_number) + ".png", "rb") as image:
        # extract binary from image
        image_bytes = image.read()

    # convert binary to base64
    img = Image.open(io.BytesIO(image_bytes))

    prompt = str(prompt) + "," + str(apend_to_prompt)

    response = stability_api.generate(
        prompt=prompt,
        init_image=img,
        start_schedule=0.6,
        steps=50,
        cfg_scale=10.0,
        width=1280,
        height=704,
        sampler=generation.SAMPLER_K_DPM_2_ANCESTRAL
    )

    print(response)

    for resp in response:
        for artifact in resp.artifacts:
            if artifact.finish_reason == generation.FILTER:
                warnings.warn(
                    "Your request activated the API's safety filters and could not be processed."
                    "Please modify the prompt and try again.")
            if artifact.type == generation.ARTIFACT_IMAGE:
                img2 = Image.open(io.BytesIO(artifact.binary))
                img2.save("videos/" + videoname +
                          "/assets/frames/3_restyled/" + str(image_number) + ".png")

def prompt_model_dreambooth(strength, folder_name,video_name, image_number, init_image, prompt, model_id, sd_api_key, apend_to_prompt, mask_image):


    sd_url = "https://stablediffusionapi.com/api/v4/dreambooth/img2img"

    init_image = upload_image("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/" + str(folder_name) + "/" + str(image_number) + ".png", sd_api_key)
        
    payload = {
        "key": sd_api_key,
        "prompt": "bukowski man. a close up of a person wearing a shirt. a character portrait. inspired by Paul Cadmus. behance contest winner. gigachad meme. in style of disney animation. smiling at the viewer. photography photorealistic. naturalistic technique. discord profile picture. blair armitage. silvio berlusconi. vladimir volego",
        # "prompt": str(prompt) + "," + str(apend_to_prompt),
        "width": "720",
        "height": "480",
        "samples": "1",
        "num_inference_steps": "20",
        "seed": "0",
        "guidance_scale": "7",
        "webhook": "0",
        "strength": strength,
        "track_id": "null",
        "init_image": init_image,
        "model_id": model_id

    }

    print(payload)

    completed = "false"

    response = r.post(sd_url, json=payload)

    while completed == "false":

        if response.json()["status"] == "processing":

            wait = int(response.json()["eta"])

            print("Processing, ETA: " + str(wait) + " seconds")

            time.sleep(wait)

            response = "https://stablediffusionapi.com/api/v3/dreambooth/fetch/" + str(response.json()["id"])

        elif response.json()["status"] == "success":

            time.sleep(3)

            output_url = response.json()["output"][0]

            image = r.get(output_url)

            with open("videos/" + str(video_name) + "/assets/frames/" + str(folder_name) + "/"+ str(image_number)+".png", "wb") as f:

                f.write(image.content)

            completed = "true"

        else:

            print("Something went wrong, trying again in 30 seconds.")

            print(response)

            time.sleep(30)

    return completed


def upload_image(image_location, sd_api_key):

    upload_success = "false"

    upload_attempts = 0

    while upload_success == "false":

        sd_api_key = sd_api_key

        sd_url = "https://stablediffusionapi.com/api/v3/base64_crop"

        # resize_image(video_name,image_number, 720, 480)

        with open(image_location, "rb") as image_file:

            image_bytes = image_file.read()

        encoded_string = base64.b64encode(image_bytes)

        upload_string = "data:image/png;base64," + \
            encoded_string.decode("utf-8").replace(" ", "")

        payload = {
            "key": sd_api_key,
            "image": upload_string,
            "crop": "false"
        }

        response = r.post(sd_url, json=payload)

        if response.json()["status"] == "success":

            upload_success = "true"

            print (response.json()["link"])

            return response.json()["link"]

        else:

            time.sleep(30)

            print(response.text)

def restyle_images(strength, folder_name,video_name, frame_number, prompt, model_id, stablediffusionapi_com_api_key, dreamstudio_ai_api_key, apend_to_prompt, mask):

    if model_id == "sd":
        prompt_model_stability(strength, folder_name,video_name, frame_number,prompt, dreamstudio_ai_api_key, apend_to_prompt)

    else:
        prompt_model_dreambooth(strength, folder_name,video_name, frame_number, str(frame_number) + ".png", prompt, model_id, stablediffusionapi_com_api_key, apend_to_prompt, mask)



def character_pipeline(index_of_current_item, project_name, key_settings, timing_details):

    print("Running character pipeline on " + str(index_of_current_item) + ".png")

    face_swap(key_settings["replicate_com_api_key"], project_name, index_of_current_item, key_settings["stablediffusionapi_com_api_key"])

    touch_up_images(project_name, key_settings["replicate_com_api_key"], index_of_current_item, key_settings["type_of_touch_up"],key_settings["custom_touch_up_query"], key_settings["dreamstudio_ai_api_key"], timing_details[index_of_current_item]["prompt"])

    resize_image(project_name, index_of_current_item, 704,512)

    # hair_swap(key_settings["replicate_com_api_key"],video_name,index_of_current_item,key_settings["stablediffusionapi_com_api_key"])

    # INSERT CLOTHES SWAP

    restyle_images(0.25,"1_character_pipeline_completed",project_name, index_of_current_item, timing_details[index_of_current_item]["prompt"], timing_details[index_of_current_item]["model_id"], key_settings["stablediffusionapi_com_api_key"], key_settings["dreamstudio_ai_api_key"], key_settings["what_to_apend_to_each_prompt"], timing_details[index_of_current_item]["mask"])

    st.experimental_rerun()

                    image = Image.open(f'videos/{project_name}/assets/resources/training_data/{image_name}') 
                    st.image(image, width=400) 
                    yes = st.checkbox(f'Add {index_of_current_item} to model')    

                    if yes:
                        images_for_model.append(index_of_current_item)
                    else:
                        if index_of_current_item in images_for_model:
                            images_for_model.remove(index_of_current_item)
                
                st.sidebar.subheader("Images for model")

                st.sidebar.write(f"You've selected {len(images_for_model)} image.")

                if len(images_for_model) < 7:
                    st.sidebar.write("Select at least 7 images for model training")
                    st.sidebar.button("Train Model",disabled=True)

                else:
                    st.sidebar.button("Train Model",disabled=False)
            
            with tab2:
                st.write("This is the tab 2")

                  

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

Based on my search results, I found information about banodoco's projects, but the results don't show specific Reddit, Twitter, or X.com discussions from 2023 about a Streamlit app with keyframe and Stable Diffusion features.

Here's what I found about banodoco's work:

Dough is a tool for crafting videos with AI, designed to give you control over video generations using a combination of images and motion settings. It allows you to guide video generations with precision using a combination of images (via Steerable Motion) and example videos (via Motion Director) with granular motion settings for each frame.

Steerable Motion, developed by banodoco, functions as a custom node within ComfyUI and leverages AI models to generate smooth video transitions between a series of images you provide. You can define keyframe positions, influence strength, image adherence, and more, allowing you to tailor the motion and style of the video.

However, my search did not specifically locate Reddit, Twitter, or X.com posts from 2023 discussing a banodoco Streamlit app with keyframe and Stable Diffusion features. The tool appears to use ComfyUI rather than Streamlit as its interface. # Banodoco Website Repository Overview

Based on the GitHub repository, here's what the README reveals:

**What it is:** "The official website for [Banodoco](https://banodoco.ai) — built with React, TypeScript, Vite, and Tailwind CSS."

**Technology Stack:** The project uses modern web development tools including React 19 with TypeScript, the Vite build tool, Tailwind CSS for styling, Framer Motion for animations, and Supabase for data management.

**Development Setup:** Contributors can get started by installing dependencies, copying environment configuration, and running the development server locally on port 5173.

**Project Organization:** The repository includes configuration files for linting, building, and styling, with architecture details referenced in a separate STRUCTURE.md document.

**Notable absence:** The README contains no information about Banodoco's history, video generation capabilities, or specific keyframes—only technical implementation details about the website itself.

The repository appears to be actively maintained (161 commits on the main branch) and is organized as a public project under the Banodoco organization.

```
# Welcome to Banodoco - V.0.2 Alpha Version!

Banodoco is a simple but powerful open source animation tool build on top of Stable Diffusion, Google FILM, and various other online machine models.

## 1) Test It Out

You can test out a preview version of Banodoco <a href="https://ba-no-do-co.streamlit.app/" target="_blank">here</a>.

While the buttons and queries won't work and some things won't display properly, it should give you a good idea of what to expect. 

## 2) Download The Repo

If you're comfortable with Git, you can pull this repo as normal. If you're not and don't want to figure that out, you can click "Code" in the top right, then click Download Zip to download all the files. 

## 3) Open Terminal

Open your terminal and navigate to the folder where you downloaded the repo.

To do this quick, you can type `cd` and then drag the folder into the terminal and press enter.

## 4) Install Dependencies

To install the dependencies, you can run the following command in your open terminal window:

`pip install -r requirements.txt`

If you're a developer, you'll probably want to install these in a virtual environment.

## 5) Run The App

To run the app, you can run the following command in your terminal window:

`streamlit run app.py`

This should open a new tab in your browser with the app running. If it doesn't, you can copy and paste the link that is printed in your terminal window.

> Note: if you encounter issues, I'd suggest that you paste the error messages you get in terminal into ChatGPT and follow its suggestions. It this doesn't work, message in Discord!

## 6) Follow The Setup Guide

Once you have the app running, you can follow the setup guide inside the app to get started!
```

import streamlit as st
import cv2
import os
import time
from PIL import Image
from moviepy.video.io.VideoFileClip import VideoFileClip
from repository.local_repo.csv_repo import get_project_settings, remove_existing_timing, update_project_setting, update_specific_timing_value

from ui_components.common_methods import calculate_frame_number_at_time, create_timings_row_at_frame_number, create_video_without_interpolation, delete_frame, extract_frame, get_timing_details, preview_frame
from utils.media_processor.video import resize_video


def key_frame_selection_page(mainheader2, project_name):
    with mainheader2:
        with st.expander("💡 How key frame selection works"):
            st.info("Key Frame Selection is a process that allows you to select the frames that you want to style. These Key Frames act as the anchor points for your animations. On the left, you can bulk select these, while on the right, you can refine your choices, or manually select them.")
    timing_details = get_timing_details(project_name)
    project_settings = get_project_settings(project_name)

    st.sidebar.subheader("Upload new videos")
    st.sidebar.write(
        "Open the toggle below to upload and select new inputs video to use for this project.")

    if project_settings["input_video"] == "":
        st.sidebar.warning(
            "No input video selected - please select one below.")
    if project_settings["input_video"] != "":
        st.sidebar.success("Input video selected - you can change this below.")

    with st.sidebar.expander("Select input video", expanded=False):
        directory_path = f'videos/{project_name}/assets/resources/input_videos'
        if not os.path.exists(directory_path):
            os.makedirs(directory_path)
        
        input_video_list = [f for f in os.listdir(directory_path) if f.endswith(('.mp4', '.mov', '.MOV', '.avi'))]
        if project_settings["input_video"] != "":
            input_video_index = input_video_list.index(
                project_settings["input_video"])
            input_video = st.selectbox(
                "Input video:", input_video_list, index=input_video_index)
            input_video_cv2 = cv2.VideoCapture(
                f'videos/{project_name}/assets/resources/input_videos/{input_video}')
            total_frames = input_video_cv2.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_COUNT)
            fps = input_video_cv2.get(cv2.CAP_PROP_FPS)
            # duration to 2 decimal places
            duration = round(total_frames / fps, 2)

            preview1, preview2, preview3 = st.columns([1, 1, 1])
            with preview1:
                st.image(preview_frame(project_name,
                         input_video, total_frames * 0.25))
            with preview2:
                st.image(preview_frame(project_name,
                         input_video, total_frames * 0.5))
            with preview3:
                st.image(preview_frame(project_name,
                         input_video, total_frames * 0.75))
            st.caption(
                f"This video is {duration} seconds long, and has {total_frames} frames.")
            # st.video(f'videos/{project_name}/assets/resources/input_videos/{input_video}')
        else:
            input_video = st.selectbox("Input video:", input_video_list)

        if st.button("Update Video"):
            update_project_setting("input_video", input_video, project_name)
            st.experimental_rerun()
        st.markdown("***")
        st.subheader("Upload new video")
        width = int(project_settings["width"])
        height = int(project_settings["height"])

        uploaded_file = st.file_uploader("Choose a file")
        keep_audio = st.checkbox("Keep audio from original video.")
        resize_this_video = st.checkbox(
            "Resize video to match project settings: " + str(width) + "px x " + str(height) + "px", value=True)

        if st.button("Upload new video"):
            video_path = f'videos/{project_name}/assets/resources/input_videos/{uploaded_file.name}'
            with open(video_path, 'wb') as f:
                f.write(uploaded_file.getbuffer())
```

            st.session_state['model'] = st.sidebar.selectbox(f"Which type of model is trained on your character?", models, index=st.session_state['index_of_last_model'])

                st.session_state['index_of_last_model'] = models.index(st.session_state['model'])
            models = ['stable-diffusion-img2img-v2.1', 'depth2img', 'pix2pix', 'controlnet', 'Dreambooth', 'LoRA','StyleGAN-NADA']            
            st.session_state['model'] = st.sidebar.selectbox(f"Which model would you like to use?", models, index=st.session_state['index_of_last_model'])                    
            if st.session_state['index_of_last_model'] != models.index(st.session_state['model']):
                st.session_state['index_of_last_model'] = models.index(st.session_state['model'])
        if st.session_state['model'] == "controlnet":   
            controlnet_adapter_types = ["normal", "canny", "hed", "scribble", "seg", "hough", "depth2img", "pose"]
            if 'index_of_controlnet_adapter_type' not in st.session_state:
                st.session_state['index_of_controlnet_adapter_type'] = 0
            st.session_state['adapter_type'] = st.sidebar.selectbox(f"Adapter Type",controlnet_adapter_types, index=st.session_state['index_of_controlnet_adapter_type'])
            if st.session_state['index_of_controlnet_adapter_type'] != controlnet_adapter_types.index(st.session_state['adapter_type']):
                st.session_state['index_of_controlnet_adapter_type'] = controlnet_adapter_types.index(st.session_state['adapter_type'])
            custom_models = []           
        elif st.session_state['model'] == "LoRA": 
            if 'index_of_lora_model_1' not in st.session_state:
                st.session_state['index_of_lora_model_1'] = 0
                st.session_state['index_of_lora_model_2'] = 0

# Welcome to Banodoco - V.0.2 Alpha Version!

Banodoco is a simple but powerful open source animation tool build on top of Stable Diffusion, Google FILM, and various other online machine models.

## 1) Test It Out

You can test out a preview version of Banodoco <a href="https://banodoco-0-2.streamlit.app/" target="_blank">here</a>.

While the buttons and queries won't work and some things won't display properly, it should give you a good idea of what to expect. 

## 2) Download The Repo

If you're comfortable with Git, you can pull this repo as normal. If you're not and don't want to figure that out, you can click "Code" in the top right, then click Download Zip to download all the files. 

## 3) Open Terminal

Open your terminal and navigate to the folder where you downloaded the repo.

To do this quick, you can type `cd` and then drag the folder into the terminal and press enter.

## 4) Install Dependencies

To install the dependencies, you can run the following command in your open terminal window:

`pip install -r requirements.txt`

If you're a developer, you'll probably want to install these in a virtual environment.

## 5) Run The App

To run the app, you can run the following command in your terminal window:

`streamlit run app.py`

This should open a new tab in your browser with the app running. If it doesn't, you can copy and paste the link that is printed in your terminal window.

> Note: if you encounter issues, I'd suggest that you paste the error messages you get in terminal into ChatGPT and follow its suggestions. It this doesn't work, message in Discord!

## 6) Follow The Setup Guide

Once you have the app running, you can follow the setup guide inside the app to get started!
```

## Found: "Banodoco" -- The Original Keyframe Tool (January 2023)

The tool you are looking for is the original **Banodoco** app, which was the predecessor to what later became known as **Dough**. It lives in this repository, which was renamed at some point in 2024:

**Repository:** [https://github.com/banodoco/Dough](https://github.com/banodoco/Dough) (originally named `banodoco/banodoco`)

### Key Facts

- **Created:** January 24, 2023
- **Author:** peter942 (Peter [REDACTED]'s GitHub handle at the time)
- **Technology:** Streamlit app (Python)
- **Version labeled in README:** "V.0.2 Alpha Version"
- **Self-description from README:** *"Banodoco is a simple but powerful open source animation tool build on top of Stable Diffusion, Google FILM, and various other online machine models."*
- **Live preview was hosted at:** `https://ba-no-do-co.streamlit.app/` (earliest) and later `https://banodoco-0-2.streamlit.app/`
- **Repository was archived:** December 29, 2024

### What It Did

The original `app.py` from the very first commit (Jan 24, 2023) reveals a complete keyframe-based video generation pipeline with these stages, presented as a Streamlit sidebar navigation:

1. **Project Settings** -- configure API keys and project parameters
2. **Train Model** -- upload training images and fine-tune Dreambooth models on Stable Diffusion
3. **Key Frame Selection** -- extract keyframes from input videos at configurable intervals (e.g., every 0.5 seconds), with buttons to nudge frames forward/backward by +/-5 or +/-10 frames
4. **Background Replacement** -- swap backgrounds on extracted keyframes
5. **Frame Styling** -- restyle keyframes using AI models with per-frame prompt, strength, and model controls
6. **Frame Interpolation** -- interpolate between styled keyframes to create smooth video

### Confirmed Stable Diffusion 1.5 Usage

In the very first commit, the code explicitly uses SD 1.5 via the Stability AI API:

```python
stability_api = client.StabilityInference(
    key=os.environ['STABILITY_KEY'],
    verbose=True,
    engine="stable-diffusion-v1-5",
)
```

The styling pipeline also used:
- **Dreambooth** fine-tuned models (via stablediffusionapi.com)
- **Replicate** API for face swapping (ghost_face_swap), face restoration (GFPGAN), and hair styling
- **ControlNet** adapters (normal, canny, hed, scribble, seg, hough, depth2img, pose)
- **LoRA** models
- **Stable Diffusion img2img v2.1** as an additional model option
- **Google FILM** (Frame Interpolation for Large Motion) for the interpolation step

### How It Evolved

By April 2023, the app had been restructured into proper modules:
- `/ui_components/components/key_frame_selection.py` -- dedicated keyframe selection UI
- `/ui_components/components/frame_styling_page.py` -- frame styling with multiple model options
- `/ui_components/components/frame_interpolation_page.py` -- video interpolation
- `/utils/ml_processor/replicate/constants.py` -- model registry with ~20+ Replicate models

The commit history shows:
- **Jan-Mar 2023:** Peter [REDACTED] (peter942) building the initial Streamlit app solo
- **Apr 2023:** piyushK52 joins development, major restructuring
- **May 2023:** "Nearing v.03" commit, "First version of zoomer" feature
- **Jul 2023:** Frame interpolation video generation
- **Aug 2023:** `magnetron` repo created (a separate data collection tool for gathering motion training data from videos)
- **Nov 2023:** `Steerable-Motion` created as a separate ComfyUI node
- **Jan-Mar 2024:** Transition to local inference (ComfyUI backend), the merge commits still reference `github.com:banodoco/banodoco` as the repo name
- **Mar 2024:** First public release as "Dough 0.5.1" (the rename from `banodoco` to `Dough` happened around this time)

### Related Repos

| Repo | Created | Description |
|------|---------|-------------|
| [banodoco/Dough](https://github.com/banodoco/Dough) (originally `banodoco/banodoco`) | Jan 24, 2023 | The original keyframe tool, later renamed Dough |
| [peteromallet/magnetron](https://github.com/peteromallet/magnetron) | Aug 4, 2023 | Streamlit-based data collection tool for gathering motion data from videos to train video generation models |
| [peteromallet/WebVid-10M-Parser](https://github.com/peteromallet/WebVid-10M-Parser) | Aug 25, 2023 | Video dataset parser (likely for training data) |
| [peteromallet/ComfyUI-AnimateDiff](https://github.com/peteromallet/ComfyUI-AnimateDiff) | Sep 5, 2023 | Fork of AnimateDiff for ComfyUI |
| [banodoco/Steerable-Motion](https://github.com/banodoco/Steerable-Motion) | Nov 11, 2023 | ComfyUI node for driving videos using batches of images |
| [banodoco/Dough-backend](https://github.com/banodoco/Dough-backend) | Jun 27, 2023 | Backend service for the app |

### Summary

The tool you are looking for is **Banodoco V.0.2 Alpha** -- a Streamlit-based, open-source keyframe video generation tool built directly on top of **Stable Diffusion 1.5** (via the Stability AI gRPC API and stablediffusionapi.com). It was created by Peter [REDACTED] on **January 24, 2023**, predating both Steerable Motion (Nov 2023) and ComfyUI Creative Interpolation. The workflow was: extract keyframes from video, select/adjust them, style them with SD 1.5 / Dreambooth / ControlNet / LoRA, then interpolate between them using Google FILM to produce the final video. The repository was originally at `github.com/banodoco/banodoco`, later renamed to `github.com/banodoco/Dough`, and was archived in December 2024.

## Model: peteromallet/There_Will_Be_Bloom

**URL:** https://huggingface.co/peteromallet/There_Will_Be_Bloom

### Model Details

- **Type:** LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation)
- **Base Model:** Wan 14b img2vid (WAN video model, image-to-video)
- **Model File:** `bloom.safetensors` (~718 MB)
- **Created:** 2025-03-28
- **Last Modified:** 2025-04-25
- **Likes:** 4
- **Access:** Public (not gated)

### Training Details

- **Training Steps:** 6,000
- **Learning Rate:** 0.00004
- **LoRA Rank:** 64

### Usage

**Trigger word:** `timlapsiagro`

**Prompt structure:**
```
timlapsiagro, Timelapse of a {what's growing} growing. {camera angle}.
```

The model generates timelapse-style videos of things growing/blooming, hence the name "There Will Be Bloom."

### Example Media

The model card contains one example video embedded directly:

- **Video URL:** `https://cdn-uploads.huggingface.co/production/uploads/630ce996b66f088d547a25cd/bmTtGA9mrfGexJlCtEZ71.mp4`

This is the only example media on the model card. There are no additional images, no community discussions, and no other uploaded examples. The author notes: "Will share data if anyone's interested."
